TROY, MICHIGAN 
TO: Board of Education 
FROM:  Richard M. Machesky, Ed.D. 
RE: Agenda Memo for the Organizational Meeting, January 19, 2016 
1. ELECTION OF ACTING CHAIRPERSON 
The first order of business is to e lect an acting chairperson who will 
conduct the meeting until such time as a president has been elected.  Past 
practice has been that the previous year’s president conducts this part 
of the meeting. 
2. APPOINTMENT OF TEMPORARY SECRETARY 
 The second order of business is for the acting chairperson  to 
 appoint Judy Holmes to act as temporary secretary until such time as the 
 secretary to the Board has been elected. 
3. ELECTION OF PRESIDENT 
The acting chairperson declares nominations in order for the office o f 
president of the board of education (please note, nominations need not be 
seconded; and a motion and support are made to close the nomination.)  
Please understand that four votes are required for election. 
4. ELECTION OF VICE PRESIDENT 
The newly elected  president declares nominations in order for the 
office of vice president, following the same procedures as outlined for the 
election of president. 
5. ELECTION OF A SECRETARY 
The president declares nominations in order for the office of secretary, 
following the same procedures as outlined for the election of president. 
6. APPOINTMENT OF TREASURER 
According to Board Policy, the treasurer is appointed annually by the 
Board as a nonvoting officer.  The assistant superintendent of business 
services has served  as treasurer of the Board.  It is my recommendation 
that this procedure continue.

     4. C.    01.19.16 
WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to time, come into 
the hands of Rick West, Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public monies 
belonging to or held for the State, County, or other political units of the State or 
otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by 
Resolution for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into 
the hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to 
be designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that said Treasurer, Rick West, is hereby 
directed to deposit and/or invest all public monies, including tax monies now in 
or coming into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the  
following depository institutions: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
Charter One Bank 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Comerica Bank 
First Merit Bank  
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be 
withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Rick 
West, and in emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Rick West, 
or the Superintendent, Richard M. Machesky, who may sign on the General Fund 
account and the Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belongi ng to the District for the 
accounts established at PNC Bank  for purposes of paying Troy Career Center 
expenses may be withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of The 
Troy Career Center Manager, Assistant Manager, or Program Coordinators; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the 
account established at Comerica Bank for purposes of paying health care and 
dependent care flexible spending claims may be withdrawn by ACH debit to Next 
Generation Enrollment, Inc., or by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the 
Treasurer, Rick West, and 
11
     4. C.    01.19.16 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Rick West, may open or close 
accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of the 
District; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested 
according to Board Policy D-1103; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine 
money from more than one fund for the purpose of making an investment 
authorized by subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School 
Code; and 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED with the above institutions is 
regulated by the school code. 
I, ______________________, Secretary of Troy School District, Oakland County, 
Michigan, certify that the Resolution was adopted by said Board at a meeting 
thereof held on the date specified above. 
Given under my hand and seal of the Troy School District in the County of 
Oakland in the State of Michigan this 19th day of January 2016.

      5. A.   01.19.16 
5.  RECOGNITION       
A.    Board of Education Recognition 
Each January, the Troy School District recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very important group of individuals: The Board of 
Education.  
Troy is truly fortunate to be governed by Board members who are 
tireless, professional, and passionate about improving the  
opportunities for our students and our families . Tonight, we would 
like to recognize our Board of Education  members with a gift  that 
represents our students and staff, the very people you work so hard 
to serve.  
This gift is a token of appreciation that will allow Board members to 
enjoy some of the talents that our students bring to the Troy School 
District, under the direction of  some very dedicated fine arts 
teachers. Each of you has a professional photograph of the mosaic 
art that was created for the new DMC -Children’s Hospital in Troy. 
The photos were printed, matted and prepared by Athens art teacher 
Luba Sordyl and represent student work from across the district. 
We hope that this will serve as a reminder of the sincere appreciation 
of your support of our Fine Arts programs. You are truly a group that 
has given so much to our students and we are grateful to have each 
and every one of you. 
Thank you for all you do.   
Recommended Action:  Present gift to Board members. 
18
     5. B.   01.19.16 
5.   RECOGNITION  
B.   TSD Mission Moment—Troy High Student Fundraising 
Tonight, we recognize a special group of Troy High students and staff who came 
together to raise money for a fellow student battl ing a life -threatening disease. 
We are  very proud of the all -out school wide effort spearheaded by Harriet 
Clark’s Personal Communication classes and Troy High’s Student Government.  
These efforts raised over $31,500 for the Karen Barrientos Cancer fund. Karen is 
an 11th grade student at Troy High with Non-Hodgkins Lymphoma.  
At this time, we would like to invite  Mr. Remo Roncone to come tell us a little 
more about these efforts. Following that, the staff and students who organized 
these projects will be asked to come up to receive recognition from the Board.

RECOGNITION 
“Flags for Classrooms” Program- 
Sons of the American Legion.  These individuals have 
committed themselves to helping our veterans, troops 
and communities in need. One of their goals is to make 
sure there is an American Flag placed in every classroom 
in Oakland County.  Mr. Randy Jackson, Chairman for 
the “Flags for Classrooms Program”, was present this 
evening to share a little about this program.  Following 
District Sons of the American Legion presented 62 
American flags to Mr. David Pass, Interim Assistant 
Superintendent for Elementary Instruction, who 
accepted them on behalf of the District.  The flags will 
be placed in designated classrooms at Baker, Boulan

Mr. Jackson’s presentation, flag carriers of the 18

Park and Larson Middle Schools as well as Athens and 
Troy High.   
The Board thanked the group for their efforts in 
organizing the program and extended their appreciation 
for the flags.  There was also some discussion on flag 
etiquette reviewing the proper way to display flags and 
disposal of flags.   
TSD Mission Moment – Troy 
the Wattles and Troy Union “Watch D.O.G.S.”  “Watch 
D.O.G.S.” stands for Dads of Great Students.  The group 
is comprised of fathers, grandfathers, uncles, and other 
father-figures who volunteer at school for at least one 
full day during the school year. “WatchDOGS” work 
alongside teachers by reading and working on flash 
cards with students, eating lunch and playing at recess, 
with students, monitoring the school entrances and 
hallways, assisting with traffic flow and any other 
assigned activities where they actively engage with all 
students. Wattles Principal Joyce Brasington and Troy 
Union Interim Principal Mike Cottone introduced the 
teachers who lead the program.  Patrick Werner, 5th 
Grade Teacher at Troy Union, Brad Sassack, 2nd Grade 
Teacher at Wattles, and Physical Education Teacher, 
Jamie VanHouzen, shared how the program has been 
working in their schools. Several dads who have 
participated in the program were also invited to speak 
about their experiences.  The students of the dads who 
participated gave “Watch D.O.G.S.” tee-shirts to Board 
members.   
The Board thanked everyone for sharing their 
experiences and agreed that it was a great program and 
appreciated their involvement with our students.

Authorization to Seek Bids for 
Property/Casualty/Liability 
Board of Education approve notification to MAISL of 
withdrawal from the Trust in order to consider 
additional bids for insurance beginning July 1, 2016. 
Troy School District understands that withdrawal from 
the pool shall be in accordance with MAISL by-laws, and 
under the terms and conditions established by the 
MAISL Pool Board of Directors.  This resolution shall be 
submitted in order to be in compliance with MAISL by-
laws and does not constitute a final decision on this 
matter. 
Mr. Schmidt asked if the pool of choices has changed 
and if the District has an opportunity to bid to a broader 
pool.  Mr. West indicated that there is a much larger 
pool than MAISL that the District would likely receive 
bids from.

Maxwell Fanroy, student representative from 
store opened in a storage room at Athens.  The 
store is like a resale shop selling gently-used items 
to students who are in need.  He also shared that 27 
students from the HOSA Club made it to the State 
Championship, which will be held in March.  He also 
mentioned that they have organized a canned food 
drive and will be collecting goods for the needy.  He 
wished everyone happy holidays. 
Snigdha Suvarna, student representative from Troy 
High School, reported that Troy High is getting into 
the holiday spirit by bonding together to make a 
difference by helping one of their fellow students 
who was diagnosed with Hodgkin’s Lymphoma.  The 
student body joined together through fundraisers 
such as Dodgeball Tournament, Rent-a-Senior, Pie 
Throwing at administrators, stopping annoying 
music and getting recognized by Fox 2 News.  They 
raised $30,000 for their classmate.   
Matt Moroski, student representative from Niles 
Community High School, reported that their dual 
enrollment program has expanded in two new 
directions.  Six students are spending their senior 
year as full-time college students and eight students 
will pilot the junior year dual enrollment program.  
29% of Niles students will be in college beginning in 
January.  He also shared that Renee Boogren, 
science teacher, received a grant from the Troy 
Foundation for Educational Excellence.  The grant 
will provide DNA materials, equipment and supplies 
for Forensic science classes.  Student Government 
students sponsored a coat drive to donate to grace 
Centers of Hope in Pontiac and they are continuing 
to raise money for the Lebanon Water Project. 
Victoria Malkoun, student representative from 
International Academy East, announced Midterms 
are this week at the IA.  She shared that following 
the Paris attacks, students from Social Justice Club, 
Black Student Association, Muslim Student 
Association, and Indian Student Association came 
together to discuss how what was happening had 
been affecting them.   She also shared that last 
week was holiday spirit week and students had a 
pajama day, beach day, white out day, and ugly 
Christmas sweater day.  She mentioned the play

that the Drama Club will be having in January and 
talked about the creative activities students are 
getting involved with.   
the Watch D.O.G.S. presentation and what a great 
program it is.  He wished everyone happy and safe 
holidays.  The rest of the Board members also

Mr. Schmidt mentioned the People of Color 
Conference he attended and how much he learned 
about diversity.  He talked a bit about what was 
covered at the conference and shared that he 
would be meeting with Dr. Machesky to debrief him 
on what he learned.   
Dr. Machesky mentioned that the Facilities Study 
Team held their first meeting and that the District 
would be hearing back from the team in early 
March.  He indicated that there are 14 members of 
the team and that Mark Dziatczak is the District 
Liaison.  He also shared that he had his first official 
visit with the Executive Board of the Troy Chamber 
of Commerce and that he is looking forward to 
connecting and working with our community. 
Dr. Philippart shared that the next meeting of the

6. B.  PERSONNEL 
CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment – Director of  
                                 Employee Services  
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Mr. David Pass to the position of  
Director of Employee  Services.  Mr. Pass has served as principal of Troy Union 
Elementary School for the past four years.  Additionally, Mr. Pass was asked to serve 
as Interim Assistant Superintendent, Elementary Instruction, during Kris Griffor’s 
absence.  Prior to coming to  Troy, Mr. Pass held several positions including, 
Assistant Principal at Avondale Middle S chool and Elementary P rincipal a R. Grant 
Graham Elementary in Avondale. Mr. Pass has a degree in Elementary Education 
from Oakland University and a graduate degree i n Curriculum, Instruction, and 
Leadership from Oakland University.  He is currently enrolled in the Education 
Specialist program at the University of Michigan with an anticipated graduation date 
of April 2016.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below:  
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by       
 Supported by      
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Board of Education approves Mr. Pass’s 
employment as Director of Employee Services  without tenure in the administrative 
position as stated above.

 10. B. 01.19.16 
10. CURRICULUM 
B. CONSIDERATION:   Weiming International Student Pilot 
Mark Dziatczak , Deputy Superintendent of Teaching and Learning,  has 
negotiated a Memorandum of Understanding (MOU ) between the Troy 
School District and Weiming Education Group.  This MOU outlines a 
Chinese and American student exchange program between Beijing 
Weiming Education Group and the Troy School District. The MOU outlines 
the opportunity for eligible Chinese stu dents from Weiming Education 
Group (Chinese Foreign Students)  to participate in a three (3 ) + one (1) 
year program wherein the eligible Chinese Foreign Students will study for 
three (3) years at Weiming schools in China and subsequently transfer to 
the Troy School District for one (1) year. Students from the Troy School 
District would also have the opportunity to study at one of Weiming’s high 
schools for summer/winter camps and/or as exchange students.  The goal 
of this program is to encourage international education for Chinese and 
American students, teachers , and administrators in order to create a 
greater understanding between the two cultures, thus preparing students 
for a global economy .  Furthermore, this program is aligned to recent 
Oakland County initiatives designed to establish economic partnerships in 
China and to promote opportunities for Oakland County businesses and 
ventures in China. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved  that the Memorandum of Understanding between 
Beijing Weiming Education Group and the Troy School District be approved under 
the Terms of this Agreement.  In the first year of this pilot program, a limited 
number of ten (10) Chinese foreign students will spend their senior year enrolled 
as students in the Troy School District.
